tests/test_librarian_duplicates.py::test_calibration_measures_text_not_titles_or_topics[Researchers described the first onboarding session as unclear because the workspace opened without any explanation of where imported notes would appear. Several participants searched for an upload control in the project header before finding it in the documents area. A visible checklist and a short example project helped them understand the sequence. They wanted to preview the destination before committing an upload and to see a clear confirmation afterward.\n\nParticipants also struggled to distinguish a collection from a project during their later session. They expected a collection to be a curated reading list that could contain selected excerpts without moving the original documents. The team recommended keeping source links visible beside each excerpt and showing exactly which items would be saved. Clear ownership labels were more useful than a second introductory tour at this point.\n\nThe final interview round focused on recovering from mistakes. Researchers wanted to undo an accepted description without losing a newer manual edit. They also wanted to compare possible duplicates side by side before deciding whether either document should be removed. Every participant preferred a review step with explicit acceptance over automatic changes to their research library.-probable_overlap] PASSED [ 31%]
tests/test_librarian_duplicates.py::test_calibration_measures_text_not_titles_or_topics[The equipment study examined maintenance scheduling for refrigerated delivery vehicles over the winter season. Drivers recorded ambient temperature at each loading stop and described the consequences of delayed replacement parts. The research team compared service intervals across several regional depots and found that inspection paperwork was often completed after the vehicle returned to the yard. A shared checklist improved consistency but did not reduce the time needed to obtain replacement components.\n\nMechanics wanted an inventory view organized by component compatibility rather than supplier name. Their central concern was preventing a truck from remaining unavailable while a readily substitutable part sat in a nearby warehouse. The team proposed testing an availability dashboard with a small fleet before expanding the program. Interviewees emphasized that accurate stock counts mattered more than an elaborate scheduling interface.\n\nA separate field observation followed drivers as they checked coolant levels and door seals before departure. These physical inspections revealed problems that were absent from the digital records. The final recommendation paired a short daily inspection with periodic review of parts consumption. This study did not evaluate software onboarding, project organization, document comparison, or research library workflows.-None] PASSED [ 31%]
